> In these days, sword fighting was not quite as sophisticated as it was at the height of the Jedi period. In this rendition Vader was, you know, half-man, half-machine, and Obi-Wan was pretty much an old man, so this fight was a hard one for them. It wasn't an acrobatic, you know, jump-around, fast fight. It was a hard fight to fight, because they're both kind of old Jedi.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #126 (2011).

In this rendition Vader was, you know, half-man, half-machine, and Obi-Wan was pretty much an old man, so this fight was a hard one for them. It wasn't an acrobatic, you know, jump-around, fast fight. > Things to Look Out For in Episode I 3D!
— Source: Star Wars Insider #131 (2012); Page contents. POV: Page contents, about Obi-Wan Kenobi.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Page contents: "Things to Look Out For in Episode I 3D!": the lightsaber used to cut through a door; the Force run; the only non-CG Jar Jar shots; the only film where Obi-Wan has no beard. (hand-read)
Our notes: `notes/materials/038-star-wars-insider/131-ins131.md:75`

What it shows (our notes): Page contents: Storyboards, "The Battle for Theed!": never-seen pre-production storyboards by Ed Natividad of a Jedi-led attack on Theed before the hangar, with Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon fighting droids (panels 1–7). (hand-read)
> The group made a daring escape once again and headed for Coruscant. Obi-Wan accompanied his master when he reported to the Jedi Council. He brought the news of the attack from the Sith Lord, and requested that young Skywalker be trained as a Jedi Knight. The Council refused to believe that the Sith had returned, refused to allow Skywalker to be trained, and assigned the two to escort the Queen when she decided to travel back to Naboo to free her people. With the help of Jar Jar Binks, the Naboo were able to form an alliance with the Gungans and together they marched onto Theed to reclaim their planet from the Viceroy. Inside the Royal Palace in Theed, as the battle for Theed raged around them, Obi-Wan and Qui-Gon were confronted by the Sith apprentice, Darth Maul. The three engaged in a fierce duel that spanned all throughout the palace’s lower levels. During the struggle, Maul got the upper hand, separating Obi-Wan 26 The Jedi Order from his master, forcing Qui-Gon Jinn to duel him one-on-one. Maul landed a fatal blow on the Jedi Master and turned to Obi- Wan to end the battle. The two locked blades, unleashing a fury of strikes against each other, but Obi-Wan proved too resourceful for the Sith Lord and sliced Maul in two.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #131 (2012); Page contents: Storyboards.

## Star Wars Insider #136 (2012)

What it shows (our notes): Page contents: Contents opener with a crop-verified quote: "Remember...the Force will be with you, always." —Obi-Wan Kenobi, A New Hope, 1977. (hand-read)
> Mara countered. “Remember, according to Parck, the only reason Thrawn came back to attack the New Republic in the first place was to whip us into fighting shape for some danger looming out there at the edge of the galaxy. Maybe he figures we’re as ready as we’re going to be and has decided to concentrate on clearing out some of the local troublemakers from his own backyard.”
>
> “Or maybe the Vagaari were more than just locals,” Luke said, feeling his stomach tighten. This was making far more sense than he cared for. “Maybe they’ve already been in contact with the threat Parck and Fel mentioned to you.”
>
> “Could be,” Mara agreed. “Of course, that would just give the Chiss one more reason to squash the Vagaari as quickly as possible. Not only would it eliminate part of the threat, but they might also learn something about possible new enemies when they sifted through the rubble.”
>
> Luke shook his head. “I wish you’d mentioned this while we were still aboard the Chaf Envoy,” he said. “We could have asked Formbi about it.”
>
> “That’s exactly why I didn’t mention it then,” Mara told him. “Because we probably would have asked, and frankly I don’t want to know. If Thrawn’s back, I think we can assume he’s more or less on our side.”
>
> She exhaled between her teeth. “If he’s not back, I guess we’ll all just have to make do on our own.”
>
> “Yes,” Luke murmured. “But we’ll do all right.”
>
> “I know.” Mara rolled onto her side to press herself closer against her husband, and Luke felt the warmth of her body and spirit flowing into his. “Because whatever it is we wind up facing, we’ll be facing it together.”
>
> He reached his arm around to stroke her cheek. Yes, they would indeed. Because whatever prohibitions and restrictions the Jedi Order had imposed on its members during the Old Republic, he knew now in the core of his being that, somehow, those restrictions no longer applied to him and his fellow Jedi. This was the New Jedi Order, and he and Mara were walking together in as perfect a harmony with each other and with the Force as he could ever expect. “The Force will be with you always, Mara,” he murmured in her ear. “And so will I.”
— Source: Star Wars Insider #136 (2012); Page contents: Contents opener with a crop-verified quote.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Survivor's Quest (Timothy Zahn), Chapter 27 (text copy `MEGA_Legend_Survivor_s_Quest_Star_Wars_Survivor_s_Quest_02_Survivor_s_Quest_Timothy_Zahn_epub`).)

> Freeze-framing reveals Vader’s arms to be robotic, as are his legs, and the 265 Chapter VII: Demons and Angels vertebrae in his neck can be seen to be artificial as well, implying that his neck was broken in his tumble into the volcano, perhaps explaining his iron-lung necessity due to complete paralysis. Those wishing to know more on this topic should consult the Star Wars Technical Commentaries, which features an in-depth medical examination on Vader’s injuries by Dr. Curtis Saxton (http://www.theforce.net/swtc/injuries.html). The Return of the Jedi novelisation contained numerous references to Anakin’s past that did not appear in any of the scripts. Obi Wan tells of The Duel: “ ‘You should not think of that machine as your father…When I saw what had become of him, I tried to dissuade him, to draw him back from the dark side. We fought…your father fell into a molten pit. When your father clawed his way out of that fiery pool, the change had been burned into him forever—he was Darth Vader, without a trace of Anakin Skywalker. Irredeemably dark. Scarred. Kept alive only by machinery and his own black will.’ ” 575 Finally, Vader’s last thoughts as he lays dying make another reference to being burned by lava: “These were memories he wanted none of, not now.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #143 (2013).

## Star Wars Insider #150 (2014)

What it shows (our notes): Page contents: Expanded Universe: Tales of the Jedi making-of, part 1 of 3, by Michael Kogge: Obi-Wan's "For over a thousand generations" lines as the greatest visual effect. (hand-read)
> Ben realizes his father was right about Vestara, but he cannot shake his feelings for her. Luke feels compelled to proceed to the Maw before Lando’s arrival, so in agreement with Lord Taalon, the Jade Shadow and several of the Sith vessels depart. Lord Taalon leaves some Sith vessels behind to await Lando, but gives them the secret order to collect material samples from the sacred site on Klatooine. This attempted raid on the fountain causes an uproar among the Klatooinians, prompting uprisings. Lando and Jaina Solo arrive at Klatooine separately, but both become involved in the turmoil as the Klatooinian elders ask for their help to sort through this crisis created by the Sith.
>
> At stake is the future of the Klatooinian people. For over a thousand generations, the Klatooinians have been enslaved by the Hutts.
>
> At stake is the future of the Klatooinian people. For over a thousand generations, the Klatooinians have been enslaved by the Hutts. In exchange for their servitude, the Hutts promised to protect their most sacred site. If the Hutts were derelict in their duty, the Klatooinians could be free of their overlords.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #150 (2014).

## The Thrawn Trilogy (Timothy Zahn): Heir to the Empire, Dark Force Rising, The Last Command, plus the Heir 20th Anniversary extras

What it shows (our notes): With no Force, Luke uses the krayt-dragon call "Ben Kenobi had used to scare the sand people". He takes his saber back from Mara's belt and cuts the vornskr in half. The vornskr tail-whips Luke and tears off Artoo's welder and grasping arm "almost casually" (ch. 26).
> “Oh, right,” she said sardonically. “The great, omniscient Jedi. See all, hear all, know all, understand all. No, we didn’t actually meet; but I was there, if you’d bothered to notice me. I was a dancer at Jabba the Hutt’s palace the day you came for Solo.”
>
> “Vader?” She all but spat the name. “Don’t make me laugh. Vader was a fool, and skating on the edge of treason along with it. My master sent me to Jabba’s to kill you, not recruit you.”
>
> "I was his hand, Skywalker ... That’s how I was known to his inner court: as the Emperor’s Hand. I served him all over the galaxy, doing jobs the Imperial Fleet and stormtroopers couldn’t handle. That was my one great talent, you see—I could hear his call from anywhere in the Empire, and report back to him the same way. I exposed traitors for him, brought down his enemies" — Heir, ch. 26
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> "The Emperor did ... He knew. And what he knew, I knew." — Heir, ch. 26 (the Emperor knew of Vader's Cloud City offer)
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> “No,” Luke said soberly. “Jabba was highly resistant to the mind-controlling aspects of the Force.”
>
> But if she had been on the Sail Barge …
— Source: Heir to the Empire — notes part 9: Chapters 26–29 (to line 3618).

What it shows (our notes): ch. 4 n7: C'baoth was originally "an insane clone of Obi-Wan Kenobi, created before the Clone Wars by the Emperor"; Lucasfilm vetoed it. Pronounced "SA-bay-oth".
> 7 In my first outline this character was an insane clone of Obi-Wan Kenobi, created before the Clone Wars by the Emperor and put here to guard the storehouse. That would have given me a very interesting dynamic to work with, especially when Luke faces him in Dark Force Rising. At that point, with his own emotions running high, Luke would have to determine whether this was a trick, or in fact a reincarnation of his old friend and Master.
— Source: Heir 20th Anniversary: Author Annotations (Zahn "TZ" and editor Betsy Mitchell "BM"), all chapters 1–32; ch. 4 n7: C'baoth was originally.

> She’d expected Skywalker to be angry. To her mild surprise, and not so mild annoyance, he wasn’t in the least. “Good,” he said encouragingly. “Very good. It’s difficult to split your attention between two separate mental and physical activities that way. And you did it well.”
>
> “The Emperor’s philosophy was to use everything you had available,” Mara said.
>
> “Somehow, that doesn’t surprise me,” Skywalker said dryly. He held out the lightsaber. “Let’s try something else. Go ahead and take the lightsaber.”
>
> “I think we’ve got time for a few more exercises,” Skywalker decided, turning back to face her. “That technique of yours is very interesting—Obi-wan never taught me anything about using the tip of the lightsaber blade.”
— Source: The Last Command: notes part 8, ch. 18 (end) to ch. 21 (Covell).

## Star Wars Handbook 3: Dark Empire (Dark Horse, 1998) + Star Wars Insider #157–159 "The Making of Dark Empire" (Titan, 2015)

> "Our basic idea is to use a framing device which could potentially enclose a lot of different stories about the subject that interests us most: the Jedi. … an outlaw monastic order or secret society charged with keeping alive the memory of 'the days when Jedi were numerous and the Force was honored and respected throughout the Republic.' … would be called, most probably, The Jedi Chronicles." — Insider #157 p. 50
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> "our first ambition is to examine the period between the first and second trilogies, when Darth Vader rose to preeminence as 'Dark Lord of the Sith'. We are told that it was during this 20 year span following the Clone Wars that Vader 'hunted down and exterminated the remaining Jedi'--all except Yoda and Obi-Wan Kenobi. … we would want to focus on the greatness of the martyred Masters, their wise and ingenious use of the Force, their awesome exploits against the Empire, and ultimately, their heroic deaths." — Insider #157 p. 50
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars Insider #157–159 (Titan, 2015): "Of Rebel Dreams and Dark Empires: The Making of a Star Wars Legend" by Michael Kogge, parts 1–3.
Context: p. 50, "Cam's Story": Kennedy's biography (Glasgow, D.C. Thomson *Commando*, *Battle*, *2000 AD*, *Rogue Trooper*, *Judge Dredd*). It reproduces the original proposal excerpt "Notes on proposed STAR WARS graphic novel":

Vader's saber "looks much like the one he used as a Jedi learner, only darker"; Luke's second saber "follows the type used by Luke's mentor Ben Kenobi". Ion blasters (Jawa ionization blasters stun droids). Blaster Weaponry: high-energy gas → plasma, "magnetic bottle effect"; "Plasma energy is dissipated as the bolt travels, limiting range". Droids: "the replication of true sentience has proven difficult and even dangerous"; droids escaping memory wipes "tend to develop identities and sentience of their own"; "In the Empire, no laws protect droids from abuse".

> We'd had so many difficulties with people trying to report stuff to the media that we called the film Revenge of the Jedi to throw people off.
>
> the first official Star Wars 'expanded universe' novel *(p. 132)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: 048 summary: Star Wars: The Ultimate Visual Guide (DK, 2005).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Production history: in the original ANH script Obi-Wan survived (p. 116). Lucas explains the ROTJ title (p. 125): "We'd had so many difficulties with people trying to report stuff to the media that we called the film Revenge of the Jedi to throw people off." Splinter was "the first official Star Wars 'expanded universe' novel" (p. 132). Dermot Power's curved-hilt Sith sketches became Asajj Ventress (p. 118).

Great Holocron: "The largest and most powerful Holocron is the dodecahedral Great Holocron". OBJECT MOVEMENT: "In his duel with Count Dooku on Geonosis, Yoda (left) used the Force to halt the fall of heavy stones ... Jedi utilize this talent not only to push, pull, and lift objects, but also to redirect projectiles and guide their starships through combat." Mind tricks: "Aliens with highly organized mental facilities [sic], such as Hutts and Toydarians, are naturally immune to Jedi mind tricks." JEDI SPIRITS: "The Sith Lord Darth Plagueis discovered it was possible to merge with the Force and still retain individual consciousness, but was not interested in the nonmaterial world. Jedi Master Qui-Gon Jinn succeeded, and later returned from the netherworld to pass his knowledge on to Yoda and Obi-Wan Kenobi". Dark Woman: original name "An'ya Kuro"; discovered "four-year-old Ki-Adi-Mundi".

A PROPHECY FULFILLED?: "Vader believes he has restored peace and justice to the galaxy and so brought balance to the Force ... BURNING RAGE: "Despite his fighting skills, Vader is defeated by Obi-Wan. With both legs and an arm severed, Vader lies helpless as red-hot lava burns his flesh and sears his lungs ... > And worst of all, in all these instances, the previous histories were denied and then covered up. It seems that with the mega-success of Star Wars, Lucas developed a complex for having to put forth the image that all had been elaborately planned by him, a years-old preparation that was finally seeing its satisfactory realization. Perhaps because he was pleased that all of his background story would finally pay off, he began to boast about how he had “detailed histories,” “voluminous notes” and “treatments on all the films”—although based somewhat on truth (he did indeed have notes on character backgrounds, as all writers do), they were gross exaggerations and very misleading, and as time went on they would grow to outright lies. A typical example comes in of interview in Alan Arnold’s book on the making of The Empire Strikes Back: “Alan Arnold: Tell me more about the overall concept of the Star Wars saga. George Lucas: There are essentially nine films in a series of three trilogies… AA: How much is written? GL: I have story treatments on all nine…Originally, when I wrote Star Wars, it developed into an epic on the scale of War and Peace, so big I couldn't possibly make it into a movie. So I cut it in half, but it was still too big, so I cut each half into three parts. I then had material for six movies. After the success of Star Wars I added another trilogy… So I'm still left with three trilogies of nine films.
>
> The saga, originally the adventures of Luke Skywalker, finally evolved into the story of Darth Vader.
>
> In Lucas's original script, Obi-Wan Kenobi (Alec Guinness) survived his duel with Darth Vader. Preproduction was already underway when Lucas decided to kill Obi-Wan, whose death dramatically increased the threat of both Vader and the Death Star.
>
> the second movement of a symphony
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Jedi was a much tougher movie to pull off
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> it was the end
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Directing is like a war
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> first major feature shot entirely on high-definition videotape
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> In IV, people didn't know whether Vader was a robot or a monster, or if there was anybody in there. This way, when you see him walk into the spaceship in Episode IV, you're going to say, 'Oh my God, that's Anakin. The poor guy is still stuck in that suit.' So the tension and drama is completely reversed.
— Source: Star Wars: The Ultimate Visual Guide (DK, 2005): Behind the Scenes, Expanding the Universe, Timeline, Index, Acknowledgments, back cover (pp. 112–144 + covers),.

Note (our notes, around the quote): p116-117 The Star Wars Saga: script begun 1972; "developed into an epic on the scale of War and Peace, so big I couldn't possibly make it into a movie. So I cut it in half, but it was still too big, so I cut each half into three parts. I then had material for six movies." "The saga, originally the adventures of Luke Skywalker, finally evolved into the story of Darth Vader." EP IV: "In Lucas's original script, Obi-Wan Kenobi (Alec Guinness) survived his duel with Darth Vader. Preproduction was already underway when Lucas decided to kill Obi-Wan, whose death dramatically increased the threat of both Vader and the Death Star." EP V: Kershner "the second movement of a symphony". Three directors. EP VI: Kasdan 2000: "Jedi was a much tougher movie to pull off"; Hamill "it was the end". Reinventing Jabba (Declan Mulholland). EP I: script begun "November 1, 1994"; "Directing is like a war". EP II: 1979 prophecy on video; "first major feature shot entirely on high-definition videotape". EP III (2005 interview) [KEY]: "In IV, people didn't know whether Vader was a robot or a monster, or if there was anybody in there. This way, when you see him walk into the spaceship in Episode IV, you're going to say, 'Oh my God, that's Anakin. The poor guy is still stuck in that suit.' So the tension and drama is completely reversed." Skywalker Ranch 1,700 → 6,500 acres.

> It couldn't be helped, Master Secura. The swamps were more dangerous than I anticipated. Not that I think anyone around here will notice.
>
> By the Force, Obi-Wan... you're supposed to be on leave! How long has it been since you've slept?
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Master Windu may not have assigned my current mission, but it's important to the war effort. Are you going to help me or not?
>
> Welcome to New Coronet, tarnished jewel of the Trigalis system.
>
> The Jedi Archives still identify this... slum as a trading post.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> But there hasn't been any legitimate business here in decades. Now, it's a pleasure pit for any murderer, pirate, spice fiend, and gambler with ties to Black Sun.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #1 (Dark Horse, November 2004): hand-read notes.

> You truly believe the war is almost over?

> Which makes them desperate, unpredictable. Dooku's commanders...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

>

> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> You're just worried about him, wandering the galaxy alone. You might be a Jedi Knight now, but he'll always be your Master.
>
> Yes. And my friend...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

> We can't spare any additional resources. Nearly every available Jedi is being diverted to the Outer Rim. You and Anakin have been allowed a brief respite only because you've been on the front lines for nearly a year -- but even you'll be needed soon. I suggest you rest.
>
> And yet committed to your search, you remain, Obi-Wan?
>
> Yes, Master Yoda. She's still alive, I can sense it... and I can bring her to justice. If I don't act, she could destroy entire armies -- wipe out entire worlds...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> We're well aware of the threat posed by Ventress. You will be permitted to continue your mission. But, be careful, Obi-Wan...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

> Anakin, every Padawan on Coruscant knows where to find you. But I don't care what you do on leave. I'm only here because I need your help.
>
> You're still chasing Ventress, aren't you? You're searching for a woman that I killed.
>
> No! Just rumors and myths! I killed her.
>
> Okay, forget Ventress. Dooku is sending an assassin -- his best assassin -- to kill a Corellian merchant. I want to stop that murder. Will you help me?
>
> No! You can call it a rescue mission, but you're still chasing a ghost! I'm going to focus on the living.
>
> Anakin... He needs you.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Every other Jedi I trust is in the thick of this war. You are my only option.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #2 (Dark Horse, December 2004): hand-read notes; p7 [KEY]: Obi-Wan.

> I'm your last choice? Now I really want to help...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> I just didn't want to take you away...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Enough. You are both Jedi Knights. You have both sworn an oath to protect the people of the Republic. If there is a murder you can prevent, you must try.
>
> Okay, fine. You're right. But I'll have to go to Theed and get my starship. Some of us respect the planet's no-fly zones.
>
> Good. I'll send you coordinates in the Karthakk system. Meet me there.
>
> You made the right decision. I'm just afraid that it will be months before I see you again. If I see you again.
>
> Just remember that, because I'll be back.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #2 (Dark Horse, December 2004): hand-read notes; p8: Anakin.
Note (our notes, around the quote): p8: Anakin: "I'm your last choice? Now I really want to help..." Obi-Wan: "I just didn't want to take you away..." Padmé: "Enough. You are both Jedi Knights. You have both sworn an oath to protect the people of the Republic. If there is a murder you can prevent, you must try." Anakin: "Okay, fine. You're right. But I'll have to go to Theed and get my starship. Some of us respect the planet's no-fly zones." Obi-Wan: "Good. I'll send you coordinates in the Karthakk system. Meet me there." Anakin: "I'm sorry..." Padmé: "You made the right decision. I'm just afraid that it will be months before I see you again. If I see you again." "I love you." Anakin: "Just remember that, because I'll be back."

> The Confederacy has pulled back to the Outer Rim. Republic forces have started a series of sieges against several key worlds. Alderaan has dealings with a number of systems there -- and I need to view the situation first-hand.
>
> Purposefully going into a war zone, Senator --?
>
> I will accept full responsibility for any consequences, Chancellor. I will have a cadre of my own troops accompanying me -- but I need an experienced Jedi General at my side. Preferably, someone familiar with the systems.
>
> If you insist on going, General Kenobi has extensive experience in the Outer Rim. He'll need young Skywalker's aid, of course.
>
> I'm afraid that's impossible. Anakin is on leave and Obi-Wan is tracking down Commander Ventress.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #2 (Dark Horse, December 2004): hand-read notes.

## Star Wars: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous (Sean Stewart, Del Rey 2004)

What it shows (our notes): Obi-Wan and Anakin on Arkania. The Jedi Serifa Altunen resigns: "I follow the Force—not the law. I serve the people—not the Senate. I will make peace—not war." / "It is the Order that has strayed from the path."
> Carefully she took off her Jedi cloak, folded it up, and handed it to Obi-Wan. “I follow the Force—not the law. I serve the people—not the Senate. I will make peace—not war.”
>
> Serifa’s eyebrows rose. “I do not feel lost. The Force is as it always has been. It is the Order that has strayed from the path.”
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 2, Chapters 4–5.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Yoda (Sean Stewart), Chapter 6 (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Clone_Wars_Series_Star_Wars_Clone_Wars_20_Yoda_Dark_Rendezvous_Sean_Stewart_epub`).)
Our notes: `notes/materials/050-yoda-dark-rendezvous/yda-02.md:26`

> "To follow your heart, to either love or hate, in the long run is the same mistake. Your judgment becomes clouded. Your motives, confused. If you are not very careful, Padawan, love will take you to the dark side. Slower than hate, yes, but no less surely for that." *(Obi-Wan, ch. 6)*
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 2, Chapters 4–5.
Context: Obi-Wan knows about the secret messages ("You just guessed." / "I am a wise and powerful Jedi Knight, you know").
Our notes: `notes/materials/050-yoda-dark-rendezvous/yda-02.md:27`

> For a long time Darth Sidious did not speak to him. Instead, he simply piped the breaking news story into Dooku’s holoconsole. A smiling Palleus Chuff, bruised but modestly triumphant. Long panning shots of the interior of the Phindar Spaceport: reporters pointing excitedly at spent flechettes and plasma scorch marks. Quickly patched holes in the floor and wall. Head shots of Master Yoda—“another glowing chapter in his legendary career.” Security footage of Trade Federation assassin droids running amok; two Jedi Knights bravely battling to save civilians before being cut down. Asajj Ventress, of course. A shot from external space station cams: Last Call tumbling heavily through space, accelerating, and then making a hyperdrive jump to certain doom. A state-of-the-art ship built at Dooku’s own expense—the third one she’d lost, if one counted the craft Anakin and Kenobi had stolen from her.
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 3, Chapters 7–8 (Phindar); *Last Call* is.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Yoda (Sean Stewart), Chapter 9 (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Clone_Wars_Series_Star_Wars_Clone_Wars_20_Yoda_Dark_Rendezvous_Sean_Stewart_epub`).)
(Full sentence/passage taken from the source text on file: `quick_tmp_ydr_stewart_epub`.)
Our notes: `notes/materials/050-yoda-dark-rendezvous/yda-03.md:32`

What it shows (our notes): Palpatine maneuvers Mace into sending Obi-Wan and Anakin to Vjun: "I think the world of Master Yoda, but he is very old, and perhaps not all that he once was."
> “Ah. Yes, I see. The politician is impressed with a victory on the battlefield of public relations; the military commander not so much. But I had anticipated you in this, at least somewhat. I tell you, Master Windu, I am not easy with Yoda’s situation for precisely the reasons you describe. I should be happier if you were to replace the fallen with another detail. I’m not sure who, exactly … Well, why not Obi-Wan? Didn’t I see in my last briefing that he had finished his last mission? Obi-Wan and young Skywalker. I would feel more comfortable if I knew they were on their way to Vjun. I think the world of Master Yoda, but he is very old, and perhaps not all that he once was. The idea of him facing Count Dooku alone, in the Count’s stronghold … it makes my blood run cold. Yes, Obi-Wan and Skywalker would do very well.”
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 3, Chapters 7–8 (Phindar).
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Yoda (Sean Stewart), Chapter 9 (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Clone_Wars_Series_Star_Wars_Clone_Wars_20_Yoda_Dark_Rendezvous_Sean_Stewart_epub`).)
Our notes: `notes/materials/050-yoda-dark-rendezvous/yda-03.md:34`

> “The students in Master Iron Hand’s class,” Obi-Wan said smoothly. “Anakin has been promising to teach them some tricks.”
>
> Obi-Wan shrugged it off, nettled. “I don’t know why I bother sticking my neck out for you.” He busied himself checking out a course for Vjun. “Especially since I feel, with every nerve in my body, that someday you won’t thank me for it.”
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 3, Chapters 7–8 (Phindar).

What it shows (our notes): Setting: Château Malreaux sits above the Bay of Tears and the River Weeping. "The first Count Malreaux had been a pirate". Vjun moss glows "a venomous green". Bitter End "had numbered some sixty thousand souls before plagues and madness had rendered it a ghost town a decade before." The acid rain rots Obi-Wan's robes ("I’d hate to be the minister for tourism here").
> Château Malreaux stood on a high bluff on the north side of the Bay of Tears, a deep-water harbor guarded by sudden shoals. The River Weeping, which ran into the bay, had hollowed out a fantastic labyrinth of caves through the coastal cliffs. These features—a harbor friendly to those who knew her secrets, and death to those who didn’t, and the chained galleries of caves that honeycombed the shore—had made the Bay of Tears the perfect smuggler’s port. The first Count Malreaux had been a pirate, extorting his grant of nobility from the surrounding territory in exchange for a promise, only occasionally broken, to stop plundering passing ships.
>
> The system of caves and tunnels that led up from the beach had exits everywhere, including, of course, the cellars of Château Malreaux. One of these underground passages opened into the side of a tall hillock, crowned with thorn-trees, half a kilometer inland. From the cover of these thorns, an interested observer watched as an old B-7 freighter, accompanied by two wasp-winged Trade Federation fighter craft, came lumbering in, apparently intending to set down on the deserted landing pads in the ruins of Bitter End, a city on the far side of the bay from the château. Bitter End had numbered some sixty thousand souls before plagues and madness had rendered it a ghost town a decade before.
>
> “What a horrible planet,” Obi-Wan remarked. “I’d hate to be the minister for tourism here.” He pointed to a magnificent manor house perhaps a kilometer inland, white stone bordered with blood red. “I think we head there. It looks to be about Count Dooku’s style, and wherever Dooku is, Yoda will be close at hand.”
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 4, Chapters 10–12.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Yoda (Sean Stewart), Chapter 10 (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Clone_Wars_Series_Star_Wars_Clone_Wars_20_Yoda_Dark_Rendezvous_Sean_Stewart_epub`).)

He has a mechanical right hand in an "elbow-length glove". He is now a Jedi Knight, and Obi-Wan sits on the Council.
> Anakin used the honorific not as learner to teacher, but as Jedi Knight to Jedi Council member. The braid that had defined his earlier status had been ritually severed after his audacious actions at Praesitlyn. His tunic, knee-high boots, and tight-fitting trousers were as black as the night. His face scarred from a contest with Dooku-trained Asajj Ventress. His mechanical right hand sheathed in an elbow-length glove. He had let his hair grow long the past few months, falling almost to his shoulders now. His face he kept clean-shaven, unlike Obi-Wan, whose strong jaw was defined by a short beard.
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 5, Del Rey back matter (text lines 2526–3870); Anakin status details: his braid was.

> In the initial stages of the war, clone troopers were treated no differently from the war machines they piloted or the weapons they fired. To many they had more in common with battle droids poured by the tens of thousands from Baktoid Armor Workshops on a host of Separatist-held worlds. But attitudes began to shift as more and more troopers died. The clones’ unfaltering dedication to the Republic, and to the Jedi, showed them to be true comrades in arms, and deserving of all the respect and compassion they were now afforded. It was the Jedi themselves, in addition to other progressive-thinking officials in the Republic, who had urged that second- and third-generation troopers be given names rather than numbers, to foster a growing fellowship.
>
> Obi-Wan regarded Anakin for a moment. “What’s more, if Chancellor Palpatine had genuine concern for your welfare, he would have kept you closer to Coruscant.”
— Source: Yoda: Dark Rendezvous: notes part 5, Del Rey back matter (text lines 2526–3870); Jedi pushed for.

## The Making of Star Wars: Return of the Jedi (J. W. Rinzler, Del Rey 2013)

What it shows (our notes): Ghost Vader. Kazanjian suggested it, then doubted it ("he’s like Hitler"). Lucas: "‘Isn’t that what your religion is all about?’" Lucas: "When Vader joins the Force he is able to retain his original identity because of Obi-Wan and the Jedi Order, because they’ve learned how to do it." Shaw: "George just said, ‘Look happy, smile.’" (p. 169).
> Ironically, Kazanjian had changed his mind about this being such a good idea. “I’d started thinking about it and I said to George, ‘Why? This guy—he’s like Hitler. He’s killed. He’s done all of these terrible things and now we’re saying he’s equal with Yoda and Obi-Wan, as if he’s gone to heaven or whatever.’
>
> And George pointed at me, he was real close, and he says, ‘Isn’t that what your religion is all about?’ And, boy, that was like being slapped on the side of the face, because, yes, it is what my religion is all about, and obviously his, but I hadn’t thought it through.”
>
> “I decided that the scene would give more closure to Luke’s relationship with his father,” Lucas would say.
>
> When Vader joins the Force he is able to retain his original identity because of Obi-Wan and the Jedi Order, because they’ve learned how to do it.
>
> George just said, ‘Look happy, smile.’ *(p. 169)*
— Source: Making of RotJ: notes part 2, ch. 2 (end) to the story-conference sidebar (pp. 43–70).

Note text that was attached to the quote line: *(p. 164)*
Note (our notes, around the quote): The throne room. The decisive revision adds Vader's line: "Sister? Your feelings have now betrayed her, too . Twins! Obi-wan was wise to hide her, but now his failure is complete. If you will not turn to the dark side, then perhaps she will." (p. 164). Lucas's long analysis: "the Emperor is the master and Vader is the apprentice ... Vader doesn’t quite understand that he’s on the chopping block ... He does it out of the super energy of wanting to protect his son, out of compassion. Vader finally has compassion for his son and realizes that his life has been a sham." (pp. 164–166). McDiarmid made "friend" "a hate word" (p. 166). The throne chair was too slow, so he moved it with his feet (p. 166).
Our notes: `notes/materials/052-making-of-rotj/rotj-02.md:137`

> The cross-eyed issue may have developed when Freeborn and his team were working on the eye mechanism so that Yoda’s eyelids could shut completely, as required for his death scene.
>
> RETURN OF THE ORIGINAL JEDI Three days over schedule, main unit greeted the great Alec Guinness on Wednesday.
>
> It was his 1st day, and Hamill’s 33rd. During breaks, Guinness could repair to a mobile home that production had rented from King’s Car Hire. Lucas had also accommodated his first Jedi by revising the script one last time, adding dialogue such as, “The other he spoke of is your twin sister. She will find it no easier than you to destroy Darth Vader.” Luke realizes that his twin sister is Leia, but Ben warns him to bury his feelings or “they could be made to serve the Emperor.”
>
> “Alec Guinness had asked for some script changes so he could say lines in his Ben Kenobi style,” says Marquand. “So George and he had worked together.
>
> of not doing anything ... of being not there ... it works beautifully for Ben Kenobi.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> “It’s a very interesting theory which I know some actors play with: of being not there. Just totally nothing, which I find very strange, but it works beautifully for Ben Kenobi.”
>
> Guinness described his stint in Jedi as “flitting across the screen” for his share of the “gold from outer space.” That Saturday, however, it had apparently taken all of Lucas’s diplomacy to bring him back just one more time—plus the promise of a discreet screen credit. “I don’t mind doing it if it only takes one morning to film, but a big credit would be unfair,” Guinness says. “I’m gone in a cough and a spit. Suppose I have a fan somewhere who has paid money to see me?”
>
> “He did feel better the second day,” Kazanjian says. “But his voice probably will have to be duped anyway.
>
> It was a dreary boring job, but I liked the director and had nothing to do with any other actors—thank God—except Mark Hamill, who is pleasant. *(p. 175)*
— Source: The Making of Star Wars: Return of the Jedi, part 3: Ch. 7 (end) and Ch. 8 (pp. 171–205).
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from The Making of Star Wars: Return of the Jedi (Enhanced Edition) (J. W. Rinzler) (text copy `MEGA_Star_Wars_Other_The_Making_of_Star_Wars_Return_of_the_Jedi_J_W_Rinzler_Enhanced_Edition_epub`).)
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from The Making of Star Wars: Return of the Jedi (Enhanced Edition) (J. W. Rinzler) (text copy `MEGA_Star_Wars_Other_The_Making_of_Star_Wars_Return_of_the_Jedi_J_W_Rinzler_Enhanced_Edition_epub`).)
(Fragment completed 2026-11-17: full sentence/passage matched word for word in the source text on file: `MEGA_Star_Wars_Other_The_Making_of_Star_Wars_Return_of_the_Jedi_J_W_Rinzler_Enhanced_Edition_epub`.)
Note text that was attached to the quote line: *(p. 175)*
Note (our notes, around the quote): Guinness's last day. Lucas spent a Saturday at his house reworking the lines, adding "The other he spoke of is your twin sister. She will find it no easier than you to destroy Darth Vader." (p. 175). Guinness had flu and didn't know his lines, so Marquand shot Hamill first. Marquand: "of not doing anything ... of being not there ... it works beautifully for Ben Kenobi." Guinness: "I’m gone in a cough and a spit. Suppose I have a fan somewhere who has paid money to see me?" To a friend: "It was a dreary boring job, but I liked the director and had nothing to do with any other actors—thank God—except Mark Hamill, who is pleasant." (p. 175).
Our notes: `notes/materials/052-making-of-rotj/rotj-03.md:7`

## Prima Official Game Guide: *Star Wars: The Clone Wars — Lightsaber Duels* / *Jedi Alliance* (flip-book)

> Also covers Jedi Alliance™ for Nintendo DS™!
— Source: Prima Official Game Guide: Star Wars The Clone Wars — Lightsaber Duels (Wii) — part 1 (print guide, full).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Front cover (cbz 1): "PRIMA OFFICIAL GAME GUIDE For Wii", with "Also covers Jedi Alliance™ for Nintendo DS™!". It shows Obi-Wan, Anakin, Ahsoka, Ventress, Grievous and Mace.
Our notes: `notes/materials/054-prima-lightsaber-duels/ld-01.md:9`

> Though he got away, Dooku did inadvertently reveal that a mysterious Darth Sidious was somehow controlling the Senate. Anakin and Amidala secretly married, but the damage had been done. Anakin had already taken his first step toward the dark side, the dark side of the Force grew stronger, and the Clone Wars began. The Clone Wars The Clone Wars The Clone Wars spanned about three years, and rapidly spread throughout the galaxy after the Battle of Geonosis. Though Count Dooku seemed the public mastermind of the Separatist strategy, he secretly answered to his Sith Master, Darth Sidious. Military actions were led by the cyborg General Grievous, and Dooku had a cadre of specialized underlings, including the bounty hunter Durge and the dark side warrior Asajj Ventress. Early in the war, Dooku’s forces mined the hyperspace routes that connected the Core Worlds to the rest of the galaxy, effectively cutting off the Republic from the bulk of its resources and allowing the Separatists relative freedom of movement in the Outer Rim. To match this maneuver, the Jedi entreated the Hutts to share their control of the Outer Rim, allowing the Republic to move their vessels through Huttcontrolled space. Over the course of the war, public opinion of the Jedi Order waxed and waned.
>
> The first battle of the Clone Wars then erupted on Geono­ sis, as the Separatist leadership scattered. In this early phase, the complicity of the various commerce entities-the Trade Federation, Commerce Guild, Corporate Alliance, Techno Union, and InterGalactic Banking Clan-was frustratingly difficult to prove, and many of the organizations were allowed to keep their Senate representation. The Clone Wars spanned about three · years, and rapidly spread throughout the galaxy after the Battle of Geonosis. Though Count Dooku seemed the public mastermind of the Sepa­ ratist strategy, he secretly answered to his Sith Master, Darth Sidious. Military actions were led by the cyborg General Grievous, and Dooku had a cadre of specialized underlings, including the bounty hunter Durge and the dark side warrior Asajj Ventress. Early in the war, Dooku's forces mined the hyperspace routes that connected the Core Worlds to the rest of the galaxy, effec­ tively cutting off the Re­ public from the bulk of its resources and allowing the Separatistsrelative freedom of movement in the Outer Rim. To match this maneu­ ver, the Jedi entreated the Hutts to share their control of the Outer Rim, allow­ ing the Republic to move their vessels through Hurt­ controlled space. Over the course of the war, public opinion of the Jedi Order waxed and waned. Their early defeats underscored their vulnerability, and their reluctant adoption of the rank ofgeneral caused them to be blamed for many of the missteps in the Clone Wars.
>
> Still, there emerged champions like Anakin Skywalker and Obi-Wan Kenobi, respectively dubbed the Hero with No Fear and the Negotiator by an approving public.
— Source: Prima Official Game Guide: Star Wars The Clone Wars — Lightsaber Duels (Wii) — part 1 (print guide, full).

## Prima Official Game Guide: *Star Wars: The Force Unleashed* (2008)

> It was not until after they met with the Jedi Council that Anakin’s dark and troubled past was brought into question. Unmoved by the council’s warnings, Qui-Gon Jinn continued informally with Anakin’s training. As the Jedi fled from the Trade Federation with Queen Amidala, they found that they were being hunted by a dark figure highly skilled with a lightsaber and shrouded in the dark side of the Force. Upon hearing of the mysterious figure’s attack on Qui-Gon Jinn, the Jedi Order realized that the Sith—a dark Order long thought to be extinct—was actually still active. Meanwhile, Senator Palpatine, a two-faced politician with dark ambitions, secretly and successfully schemed to overthrow the Supreme Chancellor and took his place. In a final move of desperation, Senator Amidala (accompanied by Qui-Gon Jinn, Obi-Wan Kenobi, and Anakin Skywalker) fled back to Naboo to help liberate her planet from the Trade Federation. Though they were supposed to protect Amidala, the Jedi had a second motive for returning. They were to draw out the Dark Lord who attacked them earlier and reveal the existence of the Sith. In a final confrontation, Qui-Gon Jinn exposed and was defeated by the Sith Lord Darth Maul, who was in turn defeated by Obi-Wan Kenobi.
— Source: Prima *Star Wars: The Force Unleashed* guide — part 1: front matter, lore primer, cast and bestiary, combat systems (printed pp. 1–35).

> Meanwhile, Anakin and Amidala grew closer while in seclusion. Though Anakin was haunted by the memory of his mother, his love for Amidala grew every day they spent together. Eventually, Anakin’s concerns about his mother overwhelmed him, and he was drawn away from the hideout in Naboo. With Amidala in tow, Anakin set out in search of his mother only to find that she’d been captured by a Tusken Raider hunting party. In a failed attempt to rescue his mother, Anakin was briefly reunited with her long enough for her to die in his arms. In a fit of rage, he single-handedly destroyed the entire village of Tusken Raiders. Meanwhile, Jango Fett led Obi-Wan to the Trade Federation planet Geonosis. There, lying amidst a large concentration of Federation ships, was a large battle droid factory. Obi-Wan infiltrated the factory to discover Count Dooku and his followers secretly scheming to destroy the Jedi and overthrow the Republic. Before he was captured, Obi-Wan sent out a call to Anakin and the Jedi Order. However, instead of following the Jedi Order’s instructions to protect Amidala on Naboo, Amidala coerced Anakin into searching for Obi-Wan in hopes of rescuing him.
— Source: Prima *Star Wars: The Force Unleashed* guide — part 1: front matter, lore primer, cast and bestiary, combat systems (printed pp. 1–35); Jango.

> Similarly, the Jedi wanted Anakin to keep watch on Palpatine, as he was gaining even more executive powers over the Republic. Things grew even more complicated for Anakin as concerns about Padmé began to trouble him. Between the fearful dreams of her death and her own concerns over the Republic, Anakin grew more frustrated and confused. When Anakin was at his most vulnerable, Palpatine subtly revealed his true motives. He played on Anakin’s fear of losing Padmé and turned him even more toward the dark side by promising to teach him a way to cheat death. As Anakin struggled with his dark side, Obi-Wan tracked down General Grievous to the Utapau system. The general, who had slain several Jedi on his own, was bested by the Jedi Master, thus bringing an end to the Clone Wars. It was then that Anakin revealed to Mace Windu that Palpatine was a Sith Lord. Windu confronted Palpatine and nearly defeated him, only to have Anakin betray him in a confused rage. Anakin had finally turned to the dark side, and Palpatine, as Lord Sidious, had full control over the former Jedi Knight. Anakin’s first act as a Sith was to execute all the remaining Jedi.
— Source: Prima *Star Wars: The Force Unleashed* guide — part 1: front matter, lore primer, cast and bestiary, combat systems (printed pp. 1–35); Obi-Wan beat Grievous.

What it shows (our notes): PROXY, "disguised as Obi-Wan Kenobi, lunges out at you". Then the personnel file on Juno: "Captain Juno Eclipse is a capable, war-hardened, highly decorated combat pilot. But you can see that she is beautiful as well."

> “Leave No Witnesses.” With your training progressing rapidly, it’s time to face your first real opponent. A Jedi named Rahm Kota is attacking a critical Imperial shipyard. You must dispatch him and return his lightsaber to your master. Do this quietly, however, because no one can learn of your existence. This means you must leave no witnesses—Rebel and Imperial forces alike. On your way to the hangar bay, your personal droid, PROXY, disguised as Obi-Wan Kenobi, lunges out at you and tries to kill you! You deflect his attack and defeat him instantly. With his training exercise complete (and failed), PROXY gets to his feet and updates you on your new pilot as if nothing had happened. According to PROXY’s personnel file, Captain Juno Eclipse is a capable, war-hardened, highly decorated combat pilot. But you can see that she is beautiful as well.
— Source: Prima *The Force Unleashed* guide — part 2: PS3/360 walkthrough, Prologue to Cloud City arrival (printed pp. 36–65); PROXY.

What it shows (our notes): Juno then rescues you
What it shows (our notes): PROXY's sacrifice: "A Jedi with shaggy brown hair and a full beard comes leaping out of the Corellian meeting room. It’s Obi-Wan Kenobi! ... Kenobi’s body disappears, revealing PROXY’s fallen form."
> The battle doesn’t last long. Kenobi strikes and Vader strikes back. A short exchange of flashing ’sabers ends with Vader’s lightsaber running through Kenobi’s chest. The Jedi Master falls to the floor. Kenobi’s body disappears, revealing PROXY’s fallen form. It wasn’t the Jedi Master after all! It was your trusty PROXY droid all along. He knew that he was no match for Vader, but his surprise attack bought you enough time to drop down from the cliff’s edge to a ledge along the cliff side. Juno swoops in to rescue you there. Not all is lost. “I’ve been waiting for you, Obi-Wan. We meet again, at last.
— Source: Prima *The Force Unleashed* guide — part 3: PS3/360 walkthrough, Cloud City to the Death Star choice (printed pp. 65–92).

> Raxus Prime Junkyard Geonosis Colosseum Kashyyyk Wookiee Kashyyyk Wookiee Village Bespin Carbonite Bespin Carbonite Chamber The challenging aspect of this arena is trying to activate your Force Unleashed mode while dealing with the Wookiee flying assault vehicle. Wait for the vehicle to swoop in over the arena’s center, then finish filling your Unleashed meter (by damaging your opponent, collecting a Force Up, or taking damage) to activate Force Unleashed mode and crash the vehicle over your rival’s head. Protected by copyright. Unauthorized or unlawful copying or downloading is prohibited. 95 Enter the Tatooine battle arena and you’ll instantly recognize whose land you’re fighting on. The tiny, tidy living quarters are befitting the spartan lifestyle of one of the only surviving Jedi. Obi-Wan Kenobi’s hovel makes for a great dueling place for fighters interested mostly in technical lightsaber combat. Sure, you can Force Throw the speeder at your foe or attempt to impale your enemy with the metal structure on the right, but aside from that, the arena is relatively empty of explosives, throwable objects, and land hazards. In fact, the sole real land hazard is only a threat while you’re Unleashed. While Unleashed, lure your opponent to the arena’s right side and bring the large cliff side crashing down on his head with one swift Force Push. As far as simple arenas go, the Sarlacc Pit is the best.
— Source: Prima *The Force Unleashed* guide — part 6: the Wii guide, Vader's Flagship to the end, Duel mode and Sith Secrets (Wii pp. 50–112); Ben's Hovel. POV: Ben's Hovel, about Obi-Wan Kenobi.

Note (our notes, around the quote): Pro-Nihilus. DarthAnt66: Nihilus's drain is "stated to be generally indefensible by Kreia, the KOTOR 2 loading screen, and Chronicles of the Old Republic." We verified the Kreia line, but it doesn't name Nihilus or drain. It's her account of being overthrown by both apprentices. The loading screen and *Chronicles* claims are unverified. So "indefensible drain" is an inference, a fair one but not a fact.

## "SWTOR Scaling - A New Perspective (2024)" (DarthAnt66)

> We’ve heard statements in the past that as a Smuggler, you’re the best in the galaxy… as a Bounty Hunter, you’re the best in the galaxy. But in an E3 interview with Dallas Dickinson, we also heard that the Jedi will just be average Jedi; thousands of them running around out there. Can you clarify this?
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> **You’re not going to be average anything in our game**. Where’s the fun in that? Why would you play the Jedi Knight if you weren’t going to be able to get the Luke Skywalker, Obi-Wan Kenobi or Anakin Skywalker experience? It has to be huge and epic! And as someone who has written for the Jedi Knight, I can tell you it’s huge… it’s epic… it’s amazing. You’re gonna get to places in the plot where you say “wait a minute, I get to do what? My quest is to do what?!” And that’s true of the Sith classes as well. The Sith classes are incredible. The Sith Warrior story is Vader plus a million. The Inquisitor – everything you like about Palpatine is there, and then some. **Our idea has always been to start from what is great about the characters from the movies, and then add as much on to that as we possibly can.**
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> So, whatever class you play, **are you the best of your breed in the galaxy?**
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> **Absolutely.** As an Imperial Agent, you’re the super best. As a Sith Warrior, the Emperor calls you personally. As a Jedi Knight, you are the last hope in the galaxy!
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

> 17 BBY Ben needed full focus vs Jedi A'Sharad Hett

Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): the core Kenobi link in the Krayt chains (verdicts.md #2 and #6 use "17 BBY Ben needed full focus vs Jedi A'Sharad Hett"). On these pages the duel runs four mostly wordless pages (p366–369). Hett has two sabers plus the gaderffii and lands a kick ("OOF!"), and they trade Force pushes. Then Kenobi takes his hand off and strips his weapons, and the duel ends decisively. Nothing on these pages says Kenobi "needed full focus"; that wording comes from elsewhere (likely the issue's prose or a guide). As drawn, the panels show Kenobi > Hett, clearly. Krayt himself calls it "MY HUMILIATION BY OBI-WAN KENOBI" (p373).

> LEGACY ERA (40+ YEARS AFTER STAR WARS: A NEW HOPE) The Yuuzhan Vong have been defeated, but the galaxy has been slow to recover from their depredations, with powerful worlds chafing at the economic burdens and military restrictions put upon them by the nascent Galactic Alliance, once-powerful species seeking to rise again, newly prosperous worlds testing their influence, and long-buried secrets coming to light. The result of all this instability is civil war. Faced with a Galactic Alliance that has fallen away from its values, Luke and the Jedi Order must decide where their loyalties lie—and so, too, must the heroes of the Rebellion.

## Darth Plagueis (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes

What it shows (our notes): [M-32] Epilogue — Sidious's TPM framing. «Maul had managed to kill a master sword fighter only to be overcome by a lesser one» L7317 → in Sidious's view, Qui-Gon (QG-32) > Obi-Wan (Padawan) as a swordsman, and Maul's loss to Obi-Wan was an upset.
> It wasn’t until he arrived in Theed and learned of Darth Maul’s defeat at the hands of the Jedi in a power-generator station that he understood in part the reason for the sense of loss and profound solitude he had experienced following the murder of Plagueis. He could have pressed one of the other Jedi who had arrived on Naboo for information as to how Maul had managed to kill a master sword fighter only to be overcome by a lesser one, but he didn’t want to know, and as a result be able to imagine the contest. Still, it gave him great pleasure to stand among Yoda, Mace Windu, and other Masters and watch Qui-Gon Jinn’s body reduced to ash, knowing that the Jedi was just the first casualty in a war that had been declared but not yet begun; one in which ten thousand Jedi would follow Qui-Gon to the grave …
— Source: Darth Plagueis (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes; [M-32] Epilogue — Sidious's TPM framing..
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Darth Plagueis (James Luceno), Epilogue (text copy `MEGA_Legend_Standalones_Star_Wars_Darth_Plagueis_James_Luceno_epub`).)
(Fragment completed 2026-11-17: full sentence/passage matched word for word in the source text on file: `MEGA_Legend_Standalones_Star_Wars_Darth_Plagueis_James_Luceno_epub`.)
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/darth-plagueis-notes.md:300`

> It wasn’t until he arrived in Theed and learned of Darth Maul’s defeat at the hands of the Jedi in a power-generator station that he understood in part the reason for the sense of loss and profound solitude he had experienced following the murder of Plagueis. > “To spare the one for the sake of the other,” she said after a moment. “Half a clan pair, this one is. And I want one to live freely, since the other can’t.”
>
> Plagueis was right, Sidious thought. I have made him prideful.
>
> The Yinchorri stratagem had failed, in any case. Additional Jedi had died, but Jedi deaths had never been the primary reason for instigating the crisis. What mattered was that Valorum had triumphed, with some help from Palpatine, it was true, but mostly on his own, by managing to bring Senators Yarua, Tikkes, Farr, and others over to his side and establishing an embargo. But with his political currency spent, Valorum’s position was more tenuous than ever. Even a hint of scandal and the Senate would lose what little confidence they had in him.
>

>
> “I didn’t inquire if the wound hurt. I asked who was responsible.”

Lethal on fodder and minor Force users, but overrated per Plagueis, and outwitted by Pavan and Garyn. «There is no pain where strength lies.» L6411 Scaling: M-33 < S-33 (disarmed); M-32 > two Crimson Corridor Jedi; Qui-Gon framed as the better swordsman than Obi-Wan.

What it shows (our notes): Feats: off-page only. He kills Maul at Theed; the Epilogue treats this as «Darth Maul's defeat at the hands of the Jedi» (L7317). Sidious dismisses him as «a lesser one» (L7317).
> It wasn’t until he arrived in Theed and learned of Darth Maul’s defeat at the hands of the Jedi in a power-generator station that he understood in part the reason for the sense of loss and profound solitude he had experienced following the murder of Plagueis. He could have pressed one of the other Jedi who had arrived on Naboo for information as to how Maul had managed to kill a master sword fighter only to be overcome by a lesser one, but he didn’t want to know, and as a result be able to imagine the contest. Still, it gave him great pleasure to stand among Yoda, Mace Windu, and other Masters and watch Qui-Gon Jinn’s body reduced to ash, knowing that the Jedi was just the first casualty in a war that had been declared but not yet begun; one in which ten thousand Jedi would follow Qui-Gon to the grave …
— Source: L7317 — Darth Plagueis (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes.

What it shows (our notes): Scaling implications: none new. Sidious's plan for him is political: «Let Obi-Wan instruct him in the ways of the Force» (L7477).
> Dooku had talent, and could be a powerful placeholder. But this seemingly guileless pleasant-faced boy, this Forceful boy, was the one he would take as his apprentice, and use to execute the final stage of the Grand Plan. Let Obi-Wan instruct him in the ways of the Force, and let Skywalker grow embittered over the next decade as his mother aged in slavery, the galaxy deteriorated around him, and his fellow Jedi fell to inextricable conflicts. He was too young to be trained in the ways of the Sith, in any case, but he was the perfect age to bond with a father figure who would listen to all his troubles and coax him inexorably over to the dark side.
— Source: L7477 — Darth Plagueis (novel, read in full): feats, scaling, quotes and story notes; Scaling implications: none new. Sidious's plan for him is political.

> Dooku looked at him as they walked. “My every instinct tells me that he was the apprentice. Obi-Wan suspects as much, as well, based on the Zabrak’s behavior. The Council is being more circumspect, but naturally they want the other one found.” He fell silent, then added, “The Sith deliberately revealed himself on Tatooine and on Naboo. > Palpatine smiled inwardly. Guide you to me, young Skywalker.

What it shows (our notes): [Statement/scaling] Anakin is quoted as calling Obi-Wan “as powerful as Master Windu” (Attack of the Clones). The article also says Anakin compared Kenobi to Mace Windu in Force power. This is a character comparison, not a demonstrated feat.
> With a thought, Anakin used the Force to lift the little globe ornament higher into the air, manipulating it all about. “Don’t get me wrong,” he remarked. “Obi-Wan is a great mentor, as wise as Master Yoda and as powerful as Master Windu. I am truly thankful to be his learner. Only …” He paused and shook his head, looking for the words. “Only, although I’m a Padawan learner, in some ways—in a lot of ways—I’m ahead of him. I’m ready for the trials. I know I am! He knows it, too. He feels I’m too unpredictable—other Jedi my age have gone through the trials and made it. I know I started my training late, but he won’t let me move on.”
— Source: Star Wars: Episode II Attack of the Clones [per Wookieepedia "Obi-Wan Kenobi/Legends" rev 15682376, ref 21; exact text match]; per Wookieepedia "Obi-Wan Kenobi/Legends" rev 15682376, ref 21; exact text match — Wookieepedia extraction: Obi-Wan Kenobi/Legends — https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Obi-Wan_Kenobi/Legends; [Statement/scaling] Anakin is quoted as calling Obi-Wan.

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Decapitated Jedi Master Glaive from behind; used Force Grip on Obi-Wan Kenobi; raised dead Gungans as an army; sliced off Padawan Zule Xiss's left arm; and nearly killed Kenobi while the Jedi were weakened by swamp gas.
